in writing. - (sv) the report deals with the social policy agenda for the next five years. it contains a series of proposals to improve the social and working conditions of our people, proposals which we wholeheartedly support. we appreciate the fact that the rapporteur uses in his text formulations which advocate a method of open consultation, which respects the independence of the member states on social policy issues, and is based on voluntary harmonization and coordination. but at the same time we are concerned that in this text we find proposals which make the commission the driving force behind the development of european social policy, which means an increase in supranationality, even in this area. if we want social and employment policy legislation to work properly, it must be the culmination of a process of evolution from the bottom up, reflecting the diversity of contexts and experiences in europe.
in writing. - (sv) we will vote against the van lancker report on the new social agenda. the commission's proposal for a new social agenda is a good document. the commission has identified a number of areas in which cooperation and harmonisation between the european union and the member states is important. the commission proposes, in this new social agenda, a framework which allows all actors to play a role and which respects the diversity of national systems. the commission will use its right of initiative and support member states in achieving the necessary reforms. the rapporteur, mrs van lancker, wants parliament to decide to amend the commission's proposal, mainly by adding certain areas for which legislation is appropriate, and secondly by defining a number of rights at european level. mrs van lancker believes that much more binding instruments should be used. the commission should not be prevented from exercising its right of initiative in areas where it has competence to act and to propose measures of a mandatory nature. our position on the european union is very positive. as members of the swedish liberal party, we see european integration as an opportunity to solve crossborder problems such as those relating to the environment, trade, crossborder movement, human rights and conflict resolution. in these areas, europe's democracies have the opportunity to show the world that cooperation leads to peace and greater prosperity. we also believe in the principle of subsidiarity, which means that decisions should be taken as closely as possible to the people concerned. that is why we are actively engaged in the question of the european constitution, which will clearly define the responsibilities of everyone. it must be absolutely clear to all citizens that the union should deal only with the issues it can best handle - the issues that go beyond our borders. all other issues must be dealt with at local, regional or national level. the commission's proposal for a new social agenda seems to us to be in line with the way we believe the eu should work. european cooperation leading to coordinated action and greater prosperity is the right approach. the european social systems are so different that mandatory legislation cannot lead to good results. we will therefore support the commission's proposal and we will therefore vote against the van lancker report on the new social agenda.
in writing. - (sv) we shall vote in favour of the resolution, but we would like to comment on the following points: the charter of fundamental rights should be a political declaration made at the nice summit. the process of unanimous decisionmaking must not only apply to matters of a constitutional nature but also to matters of fundamental importance to the member states.
in writing. - (da) the swedish and danish social democrats voted today against the report by mr olivier duhamel on the constitutionalization of the treaties. the report is based on the premise that the treaties should include a constituent element stipulating the obligation for common policies to be included as a protocol in the framework treaty and the obligation to provide for various procedures for adapting the various texts. we believe that unanimity on the fundamental questions relating to the legal basis on which the eu is based must remain the basis of all cooperation within the eu. that is why we cannot vote for the report. we do, however, share the view expressed in the report that the treaties, in their present form, leave much to be desired in terms of simplicity and transparency. we would therefore like to see the texts reworked in such a way as to allow a more rational and comprehensible approach to the objectives and means of building the eu.
in writing. - (sv) i abstained in the final vote. i consider it absolutely decisive that the eu charter of fundamental rights entails the union's accession to the council of europe convention on human rights. the word constitution is misleading, both conceptually and politically. we should rather talk about the synthesis and simplification of the eu treaty. the swedish christian democrats believe that the convention, as a working method, has not proved to be a democratic method, nor effective in its functioning, for the elaboration of a charter of fundamental rights. this applies to the knowledge of the national parliaments and their constitutional committees as well as to the european parliament's committee on constitutional affairs. in practice, the convention presidency has made its own summary of what is believed to be the conclusions of the various participants. if this convention system is to be used again in the future, its task should be limited to that of a reference group.
in writing. - (da) the swedish and danish social democrats voted today against the report by mr josé maria gil-robles gil-delgado on enhanced cooperation. we agree with the fundamental principles set out in the report, namely the need to develop enhanced cooperation within the institutional framework of the european union and the principle that the political and legal conditions established by the treaty of amsterdam for the introduction of enhanced cooperation are intended to reduce the risks of rupture of the bonds of solidarity between the member states and the risks of fragmentation of the community legal area. we recognize that a certain degree of differentiation in the pace and scope of european integration can be useful, provided that the common objectives are preserved and that differentiation is conceived as an exceptional and transitional instrument, while properly respecting the principle that it is for the countries outside - and only them - to determine whether they wish to join, and if so, when, the areas covered by enhanced cooperation. we are refusing to vote on the report because we cannot accept the proposals to change the political and legal conditions for enhanced cooperation. the abolition of the conditions under which enhanced cooperation cannot go beyond the scope of the treaty and the abolition of the right of veto and the possibility of raising this issue before the european council would constitute a violation of the fundamental principles on which european cooperation within the eu is based.
in writing. -{sv) our view of what is called flexible integration is generally positive. by allowing a group of countries to continue to deepen their cooperation in a given area, while ensuring that several member states cannot impede this flexible integration, european integration is allowed to move forward at the level of the union as a whole. as long as the common objectives of the union are promoted in the context of flexible integration, and as long as no member state is prevented from participating, this solution will appear to us to be positive and worthy of further development. however, if the future european union of 25 to 30 countries is to avoid becoming a europe à la carte, where cohesion between member states is in danger of crumbling, we believe that at least half of the member states will have to participate in this flexible integration. we are also opposed to the establishment of dual defence structures in the eu and therefore reject flexible integration in the field of foreign and security policy. after the failure of the eu in the balkans, it is absolutely urgent that the union as a whole should be able to act in the regions closest to its territory and that it should eventually be able to provide personnel and resources for conflict management and peacekeeping measures. it would be desirable to fully integrate the weu's institutional and intervention structures into the common security policy. the eu must have a credible foreign policy. we cannot rely on the united states indefinitely. at the same time, it is important to maintain this commitment of the united states, and we do not want the eu to develop a parallel mini-nato. we also do not want some member states to go further in this area, in the context of flexible integration. it is therefore important to maintain the transatlantic link, even if it is of course possible to improve the distribution and coordination of work. we believe that the union must continue to develop a common foreign policy in accordance with the present rules. decisions must be taken by majority vote, but the possibility of a constructive veto must be preserved - that is, the right for a country to choose to stay out of the way, without preventing other states from pursuing their course.

- mr. speaker, i have to go. these are three important documents which we have to discuss today and which concern one of the most important challenges we face, namely climate change. for a northerner from an area where temperatures rarely exceed 25 degrees fahrenheit [7.4°c] at this time of year, and where rain is pouring down the hills, the idea of a warming climate may at first seem appealing. it's not that simple. the climate change we face is hardly a positive thing for any of us. instead, the scenario is just as described just recently, filled with storms, rain and floods. to this must be added changes in flora and fauna. life is not going to be quite the same, and it's not going to be pleasant. the three documents on climate change which the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection has dealt with can be regarded as documents representing different time periods. the resolution on the hague conference is the most up-to-date. in this report, we in the committee on the environment present our view, and hopefully also that of the union, on what we think should happen in the hague. it has been marked by a great deal of agreement, which is a good basis for action now that the hague conference is beginning. the resolution makes clear the need for the kyoto protocol to be signed and for the industrial countries to take the lead in halting climate change. it also emphasizes very clearly that the flexible instruments should only be complementary to national measures. i am glad to hear that commissioner wallström agrees with this. i think the chairman of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection has sent a clear message that we do not think that three representatives from the european parliament are sufficient for the hague conference. i am also pleased that mr wallström says that the commission proposal is eight representatives. the european parliament and those in parliament who work on environmental issues must be taken seriously at some point, and not just used as a sort of figurehead when appropriate. it is also important to emphasize that the regular meetings and information provided by the hague conference should be constructive. it is important that the views expressed by the members of parliament are also taken into account, so that it does not become a one-way communication. the resolution on emissions trading is a good document, but it has been further improved after the amendments by the committee on the environment. the most important additions are the clear indication, as in the cop 6 document, that the trading of emission allowances itself must not exceed 50 per cent of the measures for emission reductions. it is also important that measurable targets are required for each country and for each sector. in addition, there must be legally binding measures to oblige member states to respect their quotas. there is also some concern that certain sectors are omitted from the green paper, such as the transport sector. we assume that this work will be included in the next document, that is, in the eu's strategy and action to reduce emissions, and that the transport sector will not be forgotten when we discuss climate change. the message is very clear: the principle must be that all emissions, regardless of their origin, should be covered by some control, whether in the form of emission allowances or in the form of legislation, but there should be concrete limits and controls for each type of emission. many have expressed reservations about the proposed emissions trading scheme, particularly as regards the socioeconomic implications. you're just afraid of losing your job. the fear is that companies will play off different industries against each other. i believe that we have included that doubt and that concern in paragraph 13 of this resolution, and i hope that this will reassure at least some of those who have been worried. i would like to emphasize that there is a clear error in the swedish version of this paragraph. only half of the amendments published in the swedish document have been adopted. the third document is perhaps the most important in the long term. it is about the eu's strategy and actions to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. it is also about what is really necessary, namely, changing our own life and way of life in order to achieve something. it is a welcome document. it would have been even more welcome if it had been more concrete and clear, but we hope that it will become clearer with the amendments adopted by the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection.
- mr. speaker, i have to go. as we all know, climate issues are perhaps the most intractable environmental problem of our time. it is not that we do not know what changes are needed, but that the necessary changes have such a profound impact on our whole transport and energy policy and indeed on our whole way of consuming. we are facing fundamental societal changes to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. it is the most developed countries, those with the highest economic standards and the highest per capita emissions of greenhouse gases, that have the greatest responsibility to lead the way in reducing emissions. the european union is also committed to the development of a common foreign and security policy, and the european union is committed to the development of a common foreign and security policy. responsible policies by the most developed countries must be based on their ability to reduce their own emissions. if there is to be room for emissions trading, which i very much doubt, it must be only a minor part of the overall policy. in this case, too, such trade must be strictly controlled with a view to social considerations and future development opportunities in developing countries. this system may pose very great risks in the future when russia and the developing countries are included in the emissions trading system and it becomes global. however, there are other types of flexible mechanisms that can be used with advantage at global level. the trade in emission allowances and sinks cannot replace the real changes that are needed; the same is true of nuclear power. instead, we need a strong policy for lower energy consumption, renewable energy, alternative fuels and effective environmental taxes on transport. the implementation of these measures is primarily the responsibility of the individual member states. in the field of taxation, a common minimum tax is desirable. if it is not possible to agree on a tax on greenhouse gases within the eu, countries that are prepared to act jointly should introduce such a tax anyway. as regards the moreira da silva reports, we agree with the content of most of the points, but we are more sceptical about the issue of emissions trading. such a system could be an interesting experiment at eu level, but it cannot replace other measures.
- mr. speaker, i have to go. everyone agrees on the seriousness of the climate issue, and everyone agrees that the eu must have a sensible policy to live up to the commitments we made in kyoto. i think we need to go one step further. it is not enough to have good policy in europe. i believe that the eu must take the lead in international climate action, otherwise i do not think the climate convention will be much more than theory. to take such a leading role requires an aggressive energy policy, but the truth is that the union today lacks an energy policy worthy of the name. i am myself a member of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, which is responsible for energy matters. i have repeatedly observed over the past few months that energy policy in the union is not being pursued systematically. the proposals presented are not part of a coherent strategy, but are presented without any context. one month we applaud the fact that the deregulation of the electricity market has led to lower prices. a few months later, we regret that lower prices have reduced incentives for energy efficiency and renewable energy. these are just a few examples. an offensive strategy must include a change in our energy systems. at the same time, it must also include more active support for developing countries. nevertheless, developing countries will be the ones to receive the bulk of future investment in energy production. i see that the commission is doing its best. in my view, the responsibility for the lack of results lies primarily with the member states and our governments, and is due to their refusal to accept a common carbon tax and the refusal to accept the need for a common energy policy. i wish commissioner wallström every success in her work, but i would like to end with a question: does she really believe that an offensive climate strategy can be implemented without a common energy policy?
- madam speaker, please. it is now our task as politicians to make decisions in a very difficult area of health. i say this even though my regular occupation is in the field of internal medicine. the mechanisms of action of endocrine disrupting substances are not always known. the individual variation is very great. moreover, sensitivity varies from one period to another in the human life cycle. we cannot therefore follow the usual routine and set mrls for appropriate maximum exposure, as such mrls cannot be set for endocrine disrupting substances. we must, as politicians, take the problems of endocrine disrupting substances very seriously. i think the commission's strategy paper on this is very good. we need to increase knowledge, prioritise research, speed up risk assessment and identify possible substitutes. we need the opinions of the scientific committee. the public should be given factual information. we need to consult the member states, industry and various organisations. to achieve this, it is appropriate for the commission to work on the basis of a list of priorities. i have an important objection to the draft report by the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection. i do not share the view that voluntary agreements are not a viable option in general. on the contrary, i believe that voluntary agreements can be a faster route than the legislative route. agreements such as time-limited stops, restrictions on market discharges, phasing in individual risk-free products are often preferable when the circumstances are not clear, but when flexibility and rapid action are valuable. finally, i would like to stress that legislative action must always be preceded by a comprehensive scientific assessment and recommendation by the scientific committee. these assessments and recommendations should be taken into account by us as decision-makers. it's a matter of political decency.
in writing. -{\v) in the vote on the budget we were generally against the proposal by the committee responsible to increase agricultural expenditure under heading 1. the reason is that we think that the money of the european union could be better used in other areas where it is really needed. it is absurd that almost half of the total budget of the union is spent each year on subsidizing the production and export of agricultural products. the current common agricultural policy is not only costly for the union but also contributes to maintaining high consumer prices. export aid results in the cheap disposal of surplus food products on the world market, which creates a serious competitive handicap for the agri-food industries of many countries, including developing countries. in addition, the granting of aid by the union to tobacco cultivation is a singular event, to say the least. the eu's activity and budget should instead be applied to crossborder problems that the member states are not able to solve on their own. the union's agricultural policy, which takes the form of export and production aid, must therefore be dismantled and partly replaced by aid for the preservation of biological diversity and environmental protection measures. as regards external actions under heading 4, we decided to vote on a line which does not presuppose a revision of the financial perspective. we believe that support for the pro-democracy movements and reconstruction in the balkan countries is one of the eu's priorities. it is particularly important to be able to deploy resources in the form of aid to improve the situation in serbia. it would be inappropriate to ask for a revision of the financial perspective now. if the draft budget were to be revised, we fear that eu expenditure would be increased overall, which is not acceptable at the moment. that is why parliament must work to ensure that the interinstitutional agreement is maintained and that the framework of the draft budget is respected. we think it would be more sensible to draw the funds for the balkans from the programmes under heading 4, which are unfortunately not being fully exploited today. other resources could be released through the flexibility instrument.

mr president, i would like to thank you. this is a very good report with a humanist approach, but the aim is still to stop the flow of people from and through albania. the eu has got a rather dull refugee policy, which is mostly about closing our borders. but how? why do people run? of course, they are fleeing oppression, terror and war, but they are also fleeing chaos and poverty. the desire of man for a better life tomorrow, a better life for himself, but perhaps above all for his children, is one of the most important and powerful motivating forces. we can't just try to shut out everyone who tries to get in. we need to find better ways. the hard way, the police, we can use when it comes to trafficking in children and women and slave labour. the tighter the borders we try to create, the more insane the smuggling of refugees we're going to see. of course, there is no immediate solution to the whole problem, but in the short term it is a question of creating a world of reasonable justice, of security and of opportunities for the future. this applies both to the albanian population and to the population from elsewhere who are trying to reach the prosperity that the european union appears to be through albania. our ancestors came to america to, as they believed, create a life of freedom. we try to stop those who do the same thing today. it is very sad to realize that today in western europe we are trying to create laws that would punish those who hide anne frank's unborn grandchildren. i can't believe we've gotten to this point. let's face it. we will never be able to live in peace and freedom and in prosperity and justice if we have this appalling injustice to the very nearest neighbour of the european union.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i think it would be a good thing if a new fisheries agreement could be concluded with morocco, but i still think there are major weaknesses in the text of the resolution now proposed. this is particularly true of paragraphs 3 and 4, where it is presented as a matter of course - yes, almost as a right - for the eu to fish in moroccan waters. there is no such right of course, but it is a matter of negotiating with another nation. one can imagine the reaction if morocco made similar demands for fishing in eu waters and took it for granted. i also do not think it is self-evident that the eu should finance compensation for fishermen who have fished in waters outside the european union. if you look at the eu's fisheries policy as a whole and not just at this agreement, a large part of the eu's fishing fleet's fishing outside the union is unsustainable in the long term, from an ecological perspective and from a resource perspective. fundamental changes are needed to reduce global fishing and to reduce the amount of the fisheries budget spent on agreements with third countries. most of the boats fishing in moroccan waters are spanish and portuguese, but boats from northern europe also fish there, including some swedish boats that have fished outside morocco. i read in a swedish fisheries newspaper that one of these boats is landing its catch in el aaiún, which is described as a moroccan port. however, el aaiún is not in morocco but in western sahara, which is occupied by morocco. i find it strange that boats fishing for the eu quota can land their catch in an occupied country. i would ask the commission whether these issues are being taken up in the negotiations with morocco. how are the fisheries waters of western sahara treated? and how is it possible to land a catch in an occupied country with this agreement?
- mr. speaker, i have to go. the world's largest common area of law is about to emerge. it must be based on shared values and have broad political support. the proposal to set up a first temporary eurojust unit is very welcome. the council has thus taken the first step towards institutionalising judicial cooperation, something which the ppe-de group has long called for. the rule of law requires the development of judicial cooperation in criminal matters as a counterpoint to the powers of europol. eurojust is therefore a logical step in providing citizens with a high level of security in an area of freedom, security and justice by strengthening the fight against serious organised crime. the main task of the temporary unit should be to facilitate the coordination of the work of national prosecutor's offices and to support criminal investigations in relation to organised crime. the data field requires close, clearly regulated cooperation with europol in order to avoid conflicts of competence and duplication of work. the establishment of the provisional unit is only a first step. the rapporteur believes that the experience gained should facilitate the establishment of the second stage of eurojust, which is a view which i support. the question of whether to move on to a european public prosecutor's office is another matter for discussion. i see the fight against fraud against the eu's financial interests as the role of a future european prosecutor - that role and no other. i therefore oppose, in the light of the principle of subsidiarity, a common catalogue of criminal law and the eu as a common judicial body in all criminal law - if anyone could see such a thing as relevant at the moment. what parliament has to say today is strong judicial cooperation to combat serious organised crime. that is a positive thing, and it is therefore gratifying that there is such a strong support for this in parliament. i am particularly pleased with the initiative to strengthen the protection of personal data. i congratulate gebhardt on a job well done. i would like to conclude by pointing out something that i do not think we should forget. without some cooperation, we cannot maintain the rule of law in an eu with free movement. if we want to increase the effectiveness of law enforcement, we must also strengthen the protection of human rights, which must be based on our common legal principles.
madam president, i would like to thank you. let me begin by congratulating the rapporteurs and all those who took part in the convention on behalf of the eldr group. they have shown that european affairs can be tackled in a new and exemplary way, one that is superior to the intergovernmental conference, through their democratic, open work with full transparency and opportunities for citizen participation. the fact that european cooperation is not based on economic agreements alone has been mentioned here several times. it has become particularly clear in the past year. the emergence of xenophobic and anti-democratic movements raises the question of common european values. as the european institutions gain more and more power over citizens, it is also important to set limits to this influence. the proposed charter puts the citizen and his rights at the centre in a unique way. of course, political compromises have also been made, there have been give and take, but on the whole we are faced with a catalogue of common and modern rights which clearly concretises the european community of values. as there is some concern, it is important to point out that the charter complements, and does not compete with, the council of europe's charter of human rights. it is therefore urgent to create the legal possibility for the eu to sign the council of europe convention already in nice. at the nice summit, the charter will be adopted in the form of a political declaration. this cannot be reduced to a mere ceremony of fluffy declarations. the citizens are tired of this kind of event. it must be made clear that the charter is seen as morally binding. as soon as possible after the summit, the swedish presidency must also define the procedures for working on the continuation of the charter, with the aim that, after certain amendments, it will become binding and part of a future european constitution.
mr president, i would like to thank you. the charter makes existing rights visible while making historic steps for europe. the debate on whether the eu should be based on normal rule of law is the last to be dealt with. the charter fills a gap in ec law and strengthens the protection of individuals against infringements by the european police, anti-fraud authorities and competition authorities. although we are already imposing penalties in practice, there are no normal remedies for human rights violations, which are necessary in a society based on the rule of law. every individual - woman, man, worker and entrepreneur - must be protected against any political and administrative abuse by the eu. there are no decent arguments to oppose this. the unjust victim of the legal instruments of the union must have the right to a judicial remedy. the creation of a competitive enlarged europe must be based on the rule of law. broad political support is needed for the right of individuals to seek redress for abuse. how else can a legal area of 500 million people function? the charter is a balanced and solid basis for enlargement. there are many misunderstandings about its material content and its consequences. these misunderstandings must be cleared up. for those who feel the need, i recommend that you read the explanations in convention 49, and also the case law of the two courts attached to the charter. the charter will influence the development of law in the eu and is likely to gradually gain increasing support. i believe that the court should be particularly concerned with the issues of freedom of establishment, entrepreneurship and property rights. finally, i would like to thank our competent head of delegation, mr méndez de vigo.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i've been a skeptic for a long time. the charter contains positive elements, but also concerns of principle. the most important thing for me was the call from attac, the french progressive movement, which is the fastest growing in europe, calling the charter an instrument of social regression, a tool for social disarmament. attac notes that the charter represents a step backwards compared to the rules already in force in the member states, the un, the council of europe and the ilo. attac also notes that trade union rights are ignored and considers that the real aim is to consolidate the free movement of capital. when europe's fastest growing progressive people's movement, with its roots in the homeland of the first democratic revolution in france, which cannot be suspected of any scandinavian euroscepticism or anything like that, so strongly rejects the draft charter, it is impossible for me to vote yes.
in writing. - (sv) in chapter vii, general provisions, article 51.2 of the draft charter of fundamental rights, it is stated: 'this charter does not create any new competence or task for the community and the union and does not alter the competence and tasks defined by the treaties.' that is why it really lacks impact on citizens' rights. the debate on the charter drew enthusiastic applause from supporters of a federal europe, who want to see it as an embryo of a constitution for the european union. i see a real danger in the adoption of this charter. it risks undermining the work of the council of europe on human rights and leading to a different approach to human rights in european countries in the future. i shall vote against this charter because i reject it in general. this does not mean that i am against human rights themselves. but i believe that these rights can be guaranteed by other bodies than the european union. i consider the charter proposal to be a bad text which it would be reasonable to leave aside.
in writing. - (sv) this draft text for a charter of fundamental rights contains many important formulations which give a central place to the principle of human dignity and which the swedish christian democrats therefore consider to be quite welcome. however, as the swedish municipalities and general councils are very sceptical about whether it is desirable and appropriate to make the charter legally binding, and even if the eu is able to meet all the expectations it claims to guarantee, i decided to abstain in this vote.
mr president, i would like to thank you. if turkey were a member of the eu, it would be the second most populous member state after germany. it would be the first member to geographically bridge the gap to asia, and to have a predominantly muslim tradition. unfortunately, it is a country that is not only large but also feels unstable in terms of democracy. all these factors show that turkey's application for eu membership is one of the most important issues for the future of the eu. the commission's regular report on enlargement confirms progress but also points to major shortcomings in democracy, human rights and the treatment of ethnic minorities such as the kurds. turkey has not come as far as one would have hoped. torture still exists, and the military plays too much of a political role. turkey's role in cyprus is also unacceptable. my role here is to represent the pse members on the committee on budgets. then, of course, it's about economics. from an economic point of view, poverty, inflation and budget deficits are general problems, as is the social inequality of economic progress. major structural reforms are needed here. the seppänen report is about extending the powers and mandate of the european investment bank for operations in turkey. institutionally, this issue is based on the customs union with turkey which has existed for quite a number of years. however, this customs union needs to be strengthened by a special programme of measures from the european investment bank, since turkey is not yet among the countries eligible for pre-accession aid. we consider it very important that the eu, through the eib, actively supports developments so that turkey can start a concrete process as soon as possible in order to meet the copenhagen criteria. this is why the demands of democracy and human rights must also be reflected in the eib's financing activities. the commission has also decided to extend the period of validity of the loan to the period from 1 january 2004 to 31 december 2005. although turkey is already eligible for loans in several contexts, the commission now wants to focus on economic reconstruction, particularly in the area of infrastructure, in order to strengthen the country's economic competitiveness. let me make a point of principle about the procedure, beyond the merits. this is a consultative procedure, but it would of course be reasonable to have a codecision procedure, because these decisions can actually lead to the need to adjust the budget. this is particularly true at this stage, when we are gradually increasing the use of the eib's resources, but without fully covering the financial risks we take. when the eib lends to countries outside the member states, it should also consult parliament on the political priorities to be set in this mainly foreign policy-motivated part of the bank's activities. the budgetary problem at the moment is that the margin in the guarantee fund reserve is in danger of running out. we are increasing the commitments of the investment bank, but we are not increasing the loan guarantee reserve of the eu budget by the same amount, and we have committed ourselves to financing 65% if a loan cannot be repaid. the remaining guarantee reserve is now so low that it may prevent the union from using the eib as an instrument in the event of new events requiring operations of this nature. the committee on budgets is therefore proposing that parliament approve the commission's proposal. however, we support the rapporteur's critical view that there is no effective evaluation of the eib's lending activities today, and that there is a lack of transparency and democratic control of the bank's activities. the fact that we will vote in favour is because we consider it important to contribute to a more positive development in the areas where turkey is still lacking, in this case particularly in the economic field.
mr president, i would like to thank you. paragraph 17 of the morillon report states that the european parliament 'calls on the turkish government to withdraw its occupation forces from northern cyprus'. i myself am the author of this wording in amendment no 72 adopted by the committee on foreign affairs, human rights, common security and defence policy on 10 october 2000. i would like to address myself in particular to mr verheugen, the commissioner responsible for enlargement, and to the french presidency. almost 40 percent of cyprus is occupied by foreign troops, by turkish soldiers. today, 11 years after the fall of the berlin wall, nicosia is europe's only divided capital. the island of st. paul and st. barnabas, cyprus, has not been divided historically, but has been divided for 26 years by a foreign invasion. the four freedoms of the eu cannot be applied to the whole of cyprus as a result of the partition. is it possible that turkey could even start accession negotiations without the four freedoms being applied to cyprus? should not nicosia become a united city and cyprus a united country? shouldn't the occupation forces be removed? in previous speeches the armenian genocide has been mentioned. it took place just 24 years before hitler's time and before world war ii, when hitler launched the holocaust of the jews. he himself referred to this genocide as something forgotten. turkey must face up to the truth!
mr president, mr minister, i have to say something. although i have now received explanations, i do not really understand why this imprecise and partly inhuman proposal has been put forward at all. would it not be more reasonable and appropriate for eu governments and the council of ministers to consider why people are being forced to come to europe illegally? i would like to ask the minister how many of the eu member states require entry visas today, is it 10 countries, is it 50 countries, or is it perhaps 100 countries? according to the latest data i have, it's 127 countries. is this figure correct? if so, do you think this is reasonable and do you think it is a humane asylum policy?
- without understanding how to explain such a barbaric phenomenon as honour killings, it is worth noting that dozens of turkish women are murdered every year in cold blood by their brothers, fathers, cousins or husbands, with the explicit justification that the family honour must be maintained. for the family, and especially for the men in the family, status, honor and glory are hidden in the abundance of possessions, including the female members of the family. according to this view, male family members have the authority and duty to put to death a female family member who for some reason diminishes her value as an unmarried woman. it doesn't matter if she sinned willfully or involuntarily, i.e. was raped. it is good that the council points to the initiatives taken. it is important to prohibit the reduction of sentences, but even more important is to counteract the general acceptance of honour killings. my question to the council is: are you prepared to take strong action to break the spiral of violence by, for example, demanding that muslim leaders officially renounce honor killings?
president of the court of auditors. – madam president, ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the members of the court of auditors i would like to thank you all for inviting me to present the 23rd annual report of the court. as in previous years, this presentation marks the start of the discharge procedure for the 1999 financial year. the report follows the format used for the past two years with chapters covering revenue in each of the expenditure areas under the headings of the financial perspective. you will also find a chapter dedicated to the statement of assurance, and attached to the report you will find the court's observations on implementation of the sixth, seventh and eighth european development funds. the different chapters of the annual report address four main concerns. firstly, there is an analysis of the budgetary implementation of the budgetary management practices within the specific financial headings. you will find that the court has paid more attention to the follow-up to previous observations, in response to the demands expressed by this parliament. in each of the chapters the court reports on the progress made in a number of areas on which it had reported in the past and i will return to some of these issues in my presentation. thirdly, in response to the request of the european parliament, the court has expanded its audit work on the statement of assurance and now provides additional information per area of expenditure in each chapter. finally, in each of the chapters the court presents a number of other key audit findings. many of these findings are also reported upon in more detail in the court's special reports. since the last discharge procedure the court has adopted 24 special reports containing the results of audits which focus on a wide range of specific areas in which the community's financial management can be improved. although these reports are not part of today's presentation, they are to be taken into account in the discharge procedure. the total income for the 1999 financial year amounted to eur 86.9 billion. the gross national product-based resources represent 43% of this total. the value added tax-based resources represent 36% and the traditional own resources are 15% of the total. the remaining 5% is composed of the surplus resources of the previous year and other income. this means that four fifths of the community resources, the gross national product plus the value added tax-based resources, are calculated and fixed by using statistical data. i would like to point out two major observations of the court in this context. first, for several years now, the court has commented on the use of a statistical aggregated system (sec79), which for all other purposes has already been replaced by a newer system (sec95). the difference is 16 years. further, in the context of the fight against vat fraud, the court has identified a method based on a comparison between fiscal and statistical intermediate consumption data that can be particularly useful in pointing to areas where the risk of fiscal fraud is high. it therefore invites the member states to consider the use of this method. in the customs area the court reports on the complexity and variable application of the outward processing procedure. however, it also supports the commission's proposal to modernise customs arrangements with an economic impact. with regard to the 1999 expenditure, a total amount of eur 93.6 billion was entered into the account as new commitments, and payments amounting to eur 80.3 billion were recorded. this is 96% and 92% respectively of the available appropriations for the year. the utilisation rates of the financial perspective estimates are 91% and 83% respectively. expenditure from the community budget is spread over many different areas. it is, therefore, difficult to draw general conclusions on this budgetary implementation. however, the court makes the following major points. half of the 1999 expenses are recorded under the common agricultural policy. as reported in chapter 2 of this annual report, the high rate of expenditure, which is almost 99% of final appropriations, is explained in part by numerous transfers that reflect in fact the poor quality of the initial estimates, of both revenue and expenditure. the court invites both the commission and the member states to improve the quality of these estimates. there were also numerous transfers of appropriations for the structural funds that significantly altered the structure of the 1999 budget. further, there were major differences between programme amounts and the amounts allocated by the financial perspective and the majority of structural fund interventions had to be rescheduled. in this area there was also an under-utilisation of commitment and payment appropriations and a concentration of transactions at the end of the year. in areas where the community programmes are implemented directly by the commission, such as internal policy and external actions, implementation rates are somewhat lower. there is also a heavy concentration of commitments and payments at the end of the year. this is something that we have come back to over the years. the court repeats, consequently, its comments of previous years on the negative effects of this, for instance poor and hasty decisions, heavy workload creating a risk of errors and so on. finally, in both the chapters on internal policies and external actions, the court finds that the commission's own comments on budgetary implementation are inadequate. the court welcomes, therefore, the clear commitment of the commission's answer to improve fundamentally its budgetary and accounting report. the court has produced an annual statement of assurance since the 1994 budgetary year. each year the court has expressed serious criticism of the legality and regularity of the payments and the transactions underlying them. this is the case once again. indeed, the audit of the 1999 payments revealed once again an unacceptable incidence of error which affected their amount or the reality or the eligibility of the transactions underlying them. the majority of these errors occurred in areas of the community budget that are managed by the authorities in the member states. they mainly comprise non-compliance with eligibility rules, overclaims, insufficient justification and calculation errors. there are also other failures to comply with regulations and systems weaknesses for which the effects on payments cannot be directly evaluated but which increase the risks of errors occurring. the most frequent examples here are problems in implementing regulatory control checks insufficiently, detailed supporting documentation and failure to comply with tender procedures. thus, for 1999, the court did not obtain the necessary evidence from its audit activities to ensure that the underlying transactions were legal and regular, except for own resources, commitments and administrative expenditure. on the other hand, for the sixth, seventh and eighth european development fund, the court gives a positive statement of assurance. not only is it the duty of the court to report on the errors found in its audits but we should also ensure that our audit findings are interpreted and used correctly. therefore, let me remind members of parliament that the high incidence of errors found by the court cannot, and must not, be seen as an indication of the level of fraud affecting the community budget. most of the payment errors found by the court are due to problems of inadequate financial management and control and it is only in the case of a small proportion of the errors found that further reinvestigation by olaf or the member states' authorities is justified. another risk of misrepresentation is that the objectives for corrective actions are set in terms of diminishing error rates and obtaining a positive statement of assurance. as the court already pointed out in its previous report, a medium-term improvement requires a combination of general and specific measures, including the clarification and improvement of control procedures, particularly at the level of member states. in this context, the court welcomes the commission's plan for improving financial management presented to the discharge authority in the context of the discharge for 1998. this reflection leads me to some comments on the reform of the commission. the court welcomes the efforts of the prodi commission to build a modern and robust structure of management and control and, at the same time, to focus on the way political priorities are set and resources allocated. many of the weaknesses that the commission now seeks to overcome have been emphasised by the court on many occasions. for instance, the proposals of the commission to increase the responsibility of authorising officers for the execution of commitments and payments while bringing to an end the centralised system of prior approval of transactions by the financial controller, together with the creation of an independent internal audit service, is in line with the key requirement for improved financial management that we lay down in our opinion no 4/97. another element of the reform that corresponds closely to a key priority identified by the court is the increased focus to be placed on results and performance measurement within a framework of resource allocation that is consistent with predetermined priorities and objectives that are clearly defined and measurable. at sector level, the commission has now announced reform programmes which can be seen as a response to concerns expressed by the court over a number of years, for example, the proposals for improving the management of external aid programmes. this initiative is clearly based on an analysis very similar to that of the court – most comprehensively set out in its annual report for 1997. whereas the court encourages the commission to proceed from principles to practice as quickly as is feasible, it needs to be recalled that, as yet, much of the reform is still on paper only. the implementation of this new reform will need a genuine change of the management culture of the commission, a process that will certainly take time. the annual report 1999, when reporting upon the follow-up of previous observations, shows how difficult it is for the commission to implement the needed changes which have been agreed upon. in some cases, such as the reforms of the measures for the subsidised use of skimmed milk and skimmed milk powder or the programme of assistance to south africa, as well as in certain areas of traditional own resources, the commission has taken actions to remedy specific weaknesses reported upon by the court. in other areas corrective action has been slow. for instance, for the milk quota system, the court found that the overall situation had not significantly changed and that, contrary to the court's view, increased quotas have been established, thereby giving rise to greater surpluses in the milk market and a heavy financial burden for public storage and disposal measures. in the case of the import of dairy products at preferential rates, the court considers that nothing has been done so far to avoid breaches of regulations similar to those reported in the court's special report no 4/98. in the area of the european regional development fund, the court followed up about 48 observations for which it believed a financial correction should be made. there are only 16 cases where the court is satisfied with the corrective measures taken by the commission. in the area of the social dialogue, the commission has not undertaken all the measures it had agreed to carry out in its replies to the court's observations. in the internal policies area, the court re-audited nine contracts at the transport and energy directorates-general and eight contracts at the research directorate-general. the commission had only followed up three of the 17 contracts, with actions continuing on six others. in its 1996 annual report, the court identified a number of weaknesses in the management of an export promotion fund for nicaragua and honduras. in its discharge recommendation published in 1998 the council called on the commission to review its relations with the regional bank and recover amounts unduly credited by this bank. at the end of 1999, the commission was still in the process of setting up a new organisation. from these and other examples, it is clear that the commission has hitherto not only been slow in implementing corrective measures but that it particularly faces problems when it comes to recovering unduly paid amounts. in its 2001 programme, the court has therefore planned an overall audit covering all budgetary areas of the commission's recovery procedures. as i indicated before, the commission's financial management is only part of the overall management of community funds. member states are responsible for the day-to-day management of the bulk of community funds in the areas of the common agricultural policy and the structural funds. this annual report and the special reports published since the last discharge continue to draw attention to serious and persistent weaknesses in member states' management and control systems. for instance, an important category of errors reported in the statement of assurance are irregular deductions by the central or local administrations on agricultural subsidies. also in the area of the common agricultural policy, the court calls upon member states and the commission to make checks more effectively by comprehensively applying to all common market organisations inspection procedures comparable to those of the integrated administrative and control system. the dramatic events of the recent past have clearly demonstrated the increasing importance of management and control issues on the european agenda. it should be clear to each and every one of us, whether as representatives of european institutions or of member states, that this reflects a permanent change in the values and opinions of european taxpayers. they require, in exchange for their confidence and contribution, that the european funds are being spent in a meaningful and honest way. the commitment undertaken by the commission should be seen as a constructive reply to this challenge: a reply that deserves our support. on the other hand, the commission will be aware that it has now raised high expectations and that it is obliged to put into practice its good intentions and to produce results. it rests with the budgetary authorities, which have successfully triggered the reform that addresses public financial management concerns, to continue and reinforce their efforts to ensure that this reform succeeds. in practice, they will thus have to devote sufficient attention and resources to the follow-up to, and the feedback from, the reform process. members of the european parliament will be at the forefront of this. the new president of the italian court of auditors, the corte dei conti, who was officially installed in office a few weeks ago, said in his instalment address: "i am convinced that the relations between the court of auditors and parliament would be significantly improved if parliament strengthened the instruments that are designed to ensure that parliament systematically takes results of audits carried out by the court of auditors into consideration". i make his words mine. they imply that we wish to see a development of the working organisation and methods of this parliament to reflect this need for structural change. yesterday i had the opportunity – for which i thank the president – to discuss with the president of parliament involving all the committees of this parliament in the business of financial and management control. last, but not least, we will also need the cooperation of the member states. being responsible for the day-to-day management of the bulk of community funds, the member states need to strengthen their financial controls and to take up fully their responsibilities in the fight against fraud against the community budget. only when all actors – the commission, parliament, the council, the european court of auditors and the member states – commit themselves to the reform and cooperate in this achievement will the confidence of the european taxpayer in community financing be able to be restored.
president of the court of auditors. – first of all, on the procedure for presenting the annual report to parliament and the budgetary control committee, i am happy to listen to the assurances from members of parliament that the situation this year is more satisfactory than before. i hope that we can jointly conclude that we have found the right approach to the discharge procedure. i say to mr staes that i am sorry i did not have the chance to answer all the questions that were asked in yesterday's meeting. i may also have that problem today. however, i pointed out that we are only at the beginning of this discharge procedure. i sincerely hope that we will be able to give a reasonable answer to any question that parliament or the budgetary control committee wishes to ask and that all questions that were asked yesterday and in this debate will be properly answered. i now turn to the construction of the statement of assurance and the question of figures. first of all i point out – not only to mr blak, rapporteur for this year's discharge, but to mr mulder – that we have gradually changed and developed the procedure for drawing up the statement of assurance in close collaboration with this parliament and the budgetary control committee, to meet with the wishes of the committee by including in the statement of assurance the specific observations in the different budget areas. this is a result of the discussion that we have had hitherto. i emphasise – to mr mulder, mr blak, mrs van der laan, mrs morgan and to all the other speakers who have expressed themselves on this issue – that the court will continue to develop that. but i should like to make it abundantly clear that a procedure of this kind has to develop very gradually so that we do not have a complete change in the method of data collation because then the value of this exercise would not meet the requirements we are set under the treaty. concerning the accuracy of the figure that mr blak queried: 5.5% or 3%? i do not want to sound demagogic but this question is intellectually on the same level as the question "when did you stop beating your wife". do not ask me questions that are impossible to answer. we did not produce a figure last year and we have not produced a figure this year. so the question of whether last year's figure corresponds to this year's figure is unreasonable. a statement of assurance is a very detailed exercise, including not only specific observations on the different budget areas but also a global appraisal. we will study that in detail, with your excellent secretaries. i am very happy to hear that you have staff at your disposal able to summarise the court's report in one hour. i am extremely happy. perhaps we will steal that person from you. but we will have a chance to go through all that. i will see to it that the rapporteur for the discharge is fully informed of all the details. members of the budgetary control committee point out the need for more precision. you are right that we have to be more precise in our observations. when you study this annual report you will find that what the court is trying to do is to see to it that the clearance of accounts becomes a real instrument for analysing shortcomings in implementing the agricultural policy. but a delay of more than two years makes the value of this exercise very limited. what we also point out is that, in the other area of the big bulk of spending in the member states, we do not have a procedure corresponding to the clearance of accounts. that is in the structural funds. it is very difficult to be more precise when we do not have a clearance system that would give the answers that we ask for, for instance in respect of the social fund. i pledge that we will continue, together with the committee on budgetary control and parliament, to see that we develop more precise information so as to tackle the real problem. the court entirely agrees with parliament that it is not only a question of reform of the commission or of parliament reforming its way of working with financial control. the court of auditors also has to change its working methods. now, to the question of naming: i cannot accept mr van hulten's view that this report is too political in the sense that we avoid naming the names that should be named. you are listening to a member who, as rapporteur for the committee on development, was once responsible for naming not only two countries but two companies and where the court of auditors was taken to the court of justice because of that. he implies that for political reasons we are scared to mention the names necessary for the audit that we independently find important, but i have evidence to prove the opposite. it was quite clear, in this particular case, that the court of first instance supported us by saying we not only have the right to mention these names but also a duty to do so. if you look at the report of the court of auditors on the anti-pollution measures of the commission, you will find not only countries named but also regions and the french member of the court of auditors from brittany, mr jean-françois bernicot, did not protest at the fact that we dealt with his brittany page after page. when it is relevant, from the audit point of view, we mention everything that has to be mentioned. this is the policy and principle of the court. the intention of the court of auditors is not to become a political instrument but to be an objective instrument for your political control here in parliament and we will continue to work towards this end. having said that, i pledge that we will listen carefully to parliament's requests for more precise instruments of analysis and we will ensure that we can use all these instruments to satisfy the needs of political control. i will come back to that next time when i report and give you the plan for the programme of work for next year when we see each other again. i stress, mr mulder, what i said yesterday. from a technical, statistical point of view, specific das figures on each country and major political field would require an enormous amount of personnel because when you diminish the population you increase the need for the sample so the amount of sampling will require an enormous amount of work that i do not think anyone would find feasible. that is why we should use other methods and adopt the approach that we have agreed on the specific observations field and bring the das exercise more in line with the other audits, not only of the court, but also of the commission and of the member states, in the clearance of accounts. i was also thinking about florida this morning, mr van hulten, and i am thankful that i was not responsible for auditing the election procedure in that specific state. there is always something to be happy about. i hope that the debate is over so that i can answer any question that i may have forgotten. i must comment, mr blak, on what you said. you want us to restructure the report and we have done that. i would just like to remind you that after the dialogue that we had between the committee on budgetary control, we have restructured the report. you suggested that the report should reflect the portfolios of commissioners or directors-general. i would warn against that. commissioners, like mr fischler who is responsible for executing half of the budget, must have a bigger chapter than those commissioners who have no dealings with the budget. let us be open and discuss how we will structure the reporting so that it will be as satisfactory as possible.
